### A
# Short Indication / of the Key to the Understanding of Divine Mystery.
## CONCERNING REPENTANCE.
<note>original: "DE POENITENTIA." Repentance here is not just feeling sorry for sins, but a "turning around" of the soul's orientation from the world back toward God.</note>

**W**hichever person / desires to attain to Divine contemplation <note>In Böhme’s theology, contemplation (German: "Beschawligkeit") is the mystical practice of looking inward to see the light of God within the soul.</note> within themselves / and to speak with **GOD** in Christ / let them follow this <term>process</term> <note>A "process" suggests a systematic spiritual path, often compared by Böhme to the stages of an alchemical transformation.</note>, and so they shall reach it.

1. He shall gather all his senses and reason / together with all imagination into one sense / and embrace such a strong <term>imagination</term> <note>For Böhme, "imagination" is the creative power of the human will to form an internal reality or "image" within the spirit.</note> to consider himself / what he may be : In that the Scripture of God forms him / indeed calls him a Temple of the Holy Spirit / who dwells in him / and calls him a member of Christ's body / and offers him Christ's flesh and blood for food and drink.

2. Then he shall behold himself in his life / whether he is indeed worthy of this great grace / and capable of this high title of Christ / and begin to consider his whole life / what he has done / and how he [spent] his

<meta>catchword: whole (German: "gantze"). This indicates the next page continues with the "whole time" of his life.</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a guide for achieving mystical union with God, instructing the reader to turn their senses inward and recognize themselves as a "Temple of the Holy Spirit." It marks the beginning of a practical "process" of repentance and self-examination.</summary>
<keywords>Repentance, Divine Mystery, Contemplation, Imagination, Temple of the Holy Spirit, Member of Christ, Spiritual Process, Self-Examination</keywords>

whole time [he] has spent? Whether he is also found in **Christ**? Whether he stands in the **Divine Will** / or toward what he is inclined? Whether he finds any will within himself that heartily yearns for **GOD** / and would gladly be saved?

3. And if he now finds a deeply hidden will within himself / which would gladly turn toward **God’s** grace / if only it could; let him know / that this very will is the **incorporated** / and in **Paradise** after the committed **Sin** <note>The "spoken-in Word" refers to the promise of grace (the "Seed of the Woman") that Böhme believed God breathed into the human spirit after the Fall to act as a catalyst for redemption.</note> **spoken-in Word of GOD**: that then **GOD Jehovah**, as the **Father** / draws him to **Christ**. For in our <term>constriction</term> <note>original: "Engenheit." In Böhme's thought, this describes the soul's state of being trapped in its own dark, selfish desires, lacking the "expansion" or "breadth" of divine love.</note> we have no more will toward obedience.

4. But that same drawing of the **Father** / as the **incorporated** / **spoken-in Grace** / draws all people / even the most **godless** (if he is not entirely a <note>In Böhme’s botanical metaphors, a "thistle" represents a person who has become so inherently prickly and bitter in their nature that they actively resist the "growth" of grace.</note> thistle) / and if he will stand still for a moment from his <note>original: "falsche würckung." 5. Thus no person has cause to doubt **GOD’S Grace** / if he finds within himself a desire / to convert himself one day.

6. Let that same person not delay it for a single moment / as it is written: 
> Today, if you hear the **Lord's** voice / do not harden your ears and hearts.

<summary>This page explains that the inner yearning for God is evidence of the "spoken-in Word" of grace within the soul, which acts as a divine pull toward Christ. It encourages the reader not to doubt God's mercy if they feel even a slight desire for conversion.</summary>

7. For the desire for a one-time **conversion** is God's voice within man / which the Devil covers and obstructs with his introduced images / so that it is postponed from one day and year to the next / until finally the soul becomes a thistle <note>As noted previously, the "thistle" is Böhme’s metaphor for a soul so hardened by selfishness and vice that it can no longer receive the "moisture" of God’s grace.</note> / and can no longer reach Grace.

9. These images will be: 1. <term>Pride</term> <note>original: "Hoffart." In Böhme’s view, pride is the foundational sin of the self-centered ego, seeking to elevate itself above God and others.</note> to love oneself / and to want to be honored by others. Likewise / it will be an image of power / and authority in his pride. 2. Wanting to rise in honor above others. Likewise / there will be an image of a sow <note>The sow or swine was a common Reformation-era emblem for gluttony and physical greed.</note>: Namely 3. <term>Greed</term> / which wants to have everything for itself alone /

Because we have previously written a quite detailed treatise on **Repentance** and the **New Birth** <note>Böhme refers here to his earlier writings, likely those collected in "The Way to Christ."</note>, we will let it remain here with only an indication, and direct the reader to those works, as well as to the great work on **Genesis** <note>original: "Genesin." ### Concerning the
# Mixed World <note>original: "Vermischeten Welt." In Böhme's thought, this refers to our current temporal existence where the principles of light and darkness, or good and evil, are intertwined and struggling against one another.</note> / and Its Wickedness.
### Which Is
## A Mirror / in which everyone can behold and examine himself: what kind of spirit’s child he may be.

**C**HRIST says, Matthew 23: **O** Jerusalem, Jerusalem: how often would I have gathered your children together, even as a clucking hen gathers her chicks under her wings, and you would not. Likewise: **O** Jerusalem, you who kill <note>The text here refers to the biblical passage "you who kill the prophets and stone those sent to you" (Matthew 23:37).</note>, etc. Likewise: we piped to you, and you did not dance <note>A reference to Matthew 11:17, illustrating how people refuse to respond to God’s call, whether it comes in joy or in solemnity.</note>. Likewise: what more shall I do for this stiff-necked <note>original: "halsstarrigen." A traditional biblical term for those who are stubborn and refuse to be guided by God.</note> people, who will no longer let my Spirit reprove them. Likewise: Their mouth is full of cursing and bitterness, the poison of asps is under their lips; they speak vain deceit, and their hearts are never at one. **O**, how gladly I would also eat of the best grapes: But I am like a vine-dresser <note>original: "Weingärtner."</note> who gleans after the harvest <note>This metaphor suggests a search for genuine spiritual fruit in a world where very little remains after the corruption of the "mixed world."</note>. I have become a total stranger to my mother’s children.

It serves as a spiritual mirror for readers to examine their own character and alignment with God's Spirit.</summary>

...and bears a son in her old age who shortens the days of wickedness. <note>This "son" likely represents a divine intervention or a specific prophetic figure intended to end the era of corruption described on the previous page.</note> Let this be told to you: whoever persists in their wickedness will suffer great shame for it.

Lewdness is the "Latin" on his tongue <note>The author uses "Latin" metaphorically to mean the child's primary or most fluent language; rather than learning scholarly Latin, the child is fluent in impurity.</note>: he knows all the mocking jokes to inflict upon the simple-minded. The Devil teaches them all this and rides in their heart as a lord over body and soul.

Whoever can deceive, slander, despise, or betray his neighbor, and strip him of honor and property, takes delight in it: all indecent words...

He calls for immediate repentance before the Final Judgment, dismissively addressing the rationalizations people use to justify their pursuit of worldly status and luxury.</summary>

# Epistle to a Good Friend of Jacob Böhme.

## Our Salvation in Active Love
->JESUS CHRIST<-
->in us.<-

**My** <note>The original text begins with a decorative woodcut initial 'M'.</note> very dear and **Christian** <note>original: Christlicher</note> Sir: [I wish you] high peace, alongside a heartfelt, brotherly <note>original: gliedtlicher; this term implies being a fellow "member" or limb within the spiritual Body of Christ.</note> wishing—born from desire and <term>active love</term> <note>original: würckender Liebe; Böhme uses this to describe love as a functional, energetic force that "works" or "acts" within the believer, rather than a passive emotion.</note>—that the true sun of the actual love of **Jesus Christ** might constantly rise and shine within your **soul, spirit, and body**.

**Your** <note>The original text features a decorative woodcut initial 'S'.</note> letter dated the 24th of **January** <note>original: Ianuarii</note> I received fourteen days after **Easter**; <note>original: Ostern</note> I rejoiced in it, for I see therein that you are a hungry, zealous, and desiring seeker and lover of the **true Ground**; <note>original: wahren Grundes; in Böhme’s mystical theology, the "Ground" refers to the underlying divine essence or the foundation of all reality.</note> which, as I understand, you have diligently pursued and researched.

<summary>Jacob Böhme begins a letter to a friend, offering a blessing of "active love" and acknowledging the recipient's diligent spiritual search for the divine "Ground."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, active love, Jesus Christ, soul spirit and body, the Ground, spiritual seeking, epistle</keywords>

--- Page 246 ---
<meta>This page continues the letter from Jacob Böhme to a friend, expanding on the spiritual journey of the soul and the limitations of human reason.</meta>

<meta>catchword: through [original: durch]</meta>

That my writings have also come into his hands and that he took pleasure in them is certainly the cause and a **Divine Order** <note>original: Göttliche Ordnung; Böhme refers to a sense of divine providence or arrangement.</note> which leads those who love toward Love and often uses strange means through which He fulfills the desire of those who love, by which He feeds them with His gift and leads their love’s **essence** <term>Ens</term> <note>original: Ens; a term used by Böhme and Paracelsus to describe the spiritual substance or the "being-ness" of a thing.</note> to burn as a true fire: and the gentleman should certainly believe, so long as he remains constant in his love for the truth, that it will open itself to him in fire-flaming love and make itself known rightly.

Only let it be started rightly: For we do not attain the true ground of **Divine Knowledge** <note>original: Göttlicher Erkändtnüß</note> through our sharp reason and searching. The searching must begin from within, in the hunger of the soul: For the searching of reason reaches only into its **astral influence** <term>Astrum</term> <note>original: Astrum; Böhme uses this Latin term to describe the celestial or "starry" forces that govern the physical mind and the outer world.</note> of the outer world, from which reason originates <note>original: vrständet; an archaic term meaning "to take its standing" or to have its source of existence.</note>. But the soul searches in its own **astral influence** <term>Astro</term>, as in the inner spiritual world from which the visible world originated or flowed out, and in which it stands with its foundation.

But if the soul wishes to search its own **astral influence** <term>Astrum</term>, as the **Great Mystery** <term>Mysterium Magnum</term> <note>original: Mysterium Magnum; the "Great Mystery" refers to the divine chaos or the "nothing" from which God brought forth all things.</note>, it must first completely surrender all its power and will to Divine Love and Grace, and first become as a child, and

<meta>catchword: through [original: durch]</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that true spiritual understanding is a gift of Divine Order and cannot be reached by intellectual reason alone. He argues that the soul must look past the "outer" world and surrender to God's love to understand the "Great Mystery" of its own spiritual origin.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Order, spiritual essence, Ens, Divine Knowledge, Reason vs Soul, Astrum, Great Mystery, Mysterium Magnum, spiritual surrender, inner world</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Jacob Böhme’s letter regarding the soul's search for divine truth, following the previous page's discussion on the limitations of reason and the "Great Mystery."</meta>

<page-num>241</page-num>

Through great **repentance** <note>original: Busse; in Böhme’s theology, this is not just being sorry for sins, but a fundamental turning of the will back toward God.</note> / turn toward their **center** <term>Center</term> <note>original: Centro; the inward spiritual point or "heart" where the soul meets the divine light.</note> / and wish to do nothing / except what the Spirit of God wishes to search through them. And when the soul has thus surrendered itself / and thereby seeks nothing but God’s and its own **salvation** <note>original: Seeligkeit; also carries the meaning of blessedness or spiritual bliss.</note> / along with the service and love of the neighbor / and finds itself then in such a desire / that it would gladly have Divine and Natural **knowledge** <note>original: Erkäntnüss</note> / then it should know / that it is drawn toward it by God / then it may well search such a deep ground / as is indicated in my writings.

For the Spirit of God searches through the soul / and leads it finally into the depth of the **Godhead** <term>Godhead</term> <note>original: Gottheit; the essential, infinite nature of God.</note> / as **St. Paul** <note>original: S. Paulus</note> says: 
> The Spirit searches all things / even the depth of the Godhead.

My dear heart / it is quite a simple **children’s path** <note>original: Kinder-Weg; Böhme’s term for a path of humble, child-like faith and simplicity, contrasted with the "sharp reason" of scholars.</note> to the highest wisdom / the world does not know it : You do not need to seek it in far-off places. It stands before your soul’s door / and knocks : If it is / that it may find a vacant / **surrendered** <note>original: gelassene; relating to "Gelassenheit," the mystical practice of letting go of one's own will to let God act through the soul.</note> place in the soul / it will surely reveal itself there / and rejoice more therein than the sun in the elements. If the soul gives itself over to Wisdom as its own possession / then Wisdom permeates the soul with its fire-flaming love / and unlocks all **secrets** <note>original: Geheimnüss; often translated as "mysteries" in Böhme’s works.</note> for it.

<summary>Böhme describes the "children's path" to wisdom, explaining that through repentance and surrender (Gelassenheit), the soul becomes a vessel for the Spirit of God to reveal deep divine mysteries.</summary>

--- Page 248 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Böhme’s letter to a correspondent, explaining that spiritual understanding comes not from academic study but from the internal illumination of the Holy Spirit, which he calls the "School of Pentecost."</meta>

<page-num>242</page-num>

It might perhaps surprise the gentleman; how a layman <note>original: Leye; a person without formal university training or clerical status.</note> understands such high things? One who has never learned nor read them. But I tell you, my dear sir, that up until now you have seen only a glimmer in my writings of such **mysteries** <note>original: Geheimnussen</note>: For one cannot write it down: If you were to be recognized as worthy by God, so that the light would be kindled in your soul, then you would hear, taste, smell, feel, and see the ineffable words of God from such **knowledge** <note>original: Erkäntnuß</note>. Only then is the true <term>Theosophical School of Pentecost</term> <note>original: Theosophische Pfingstschuele; Böhme refers to a direct, mystical revelation from the Holy Spirit, similar to the experience of the Apostles at Pentecost, rather than learning from books.</note>: where the soul is taught by God.

Afterwards, no searching or sharp effort is required: all the gates stand open. Even a very simple person can reach it, provided he does not resist it himself with his own willing and running: for it already lies within man, and only needs to be awakened by God’s Spirit.

In my **talent** <term>Talent</term> <note>original: Talento; referring to the biblical Parable of the Talents, meaning the spiritual gift or "vocation" God entrusted to him.</note>, as I have been able to write it down in simplicity, you will clearly see the way to this; especially in this attached **little book** <note>original: Büchlein</note>, which is also of my talent, and was sent to print only a few weeks ago. Which I present to the gentleman in love, as my Christian fellow-member, and [urge] him often to read <note>The text ends mid-sentence; the catchword from the original manuscript is "lesen" (to read).</note>

<summary>Böhme explains that divine wisdom is an internal experience of the Holy Spirit available even to the unlearned, rather than a product of academic study. He presents his correspondent with a recently printed treatise as a guide to this path.</summary>
<keywords>layman, illumination, School of Pentecost, spiritual senses, divine gift, simplicity, internal awakening</keywords>

[I] urge [him] to read: For its virtue is called "the longer, the dearer" <note>An old German idiom (*je länger je lieber*) meaning that something becomes more precious the longer one possesses or engages with it.</note>. <meta>This page (230) continues the sharp social and spiritual critique from page 229. While the previous page described the "superior" or ruler devising schemes to seize power, this page details the consequences of that exploitation: the dehumanization of the servant and the subsequent corruption of the lower classes who mimic the vices of their masters.</meta>

...has more than one single soul as **property** <note>original: *Eygenthum*; here referring to the hubris of rulers who claim ownership over the lives and souls of their subjects.</note> and is only a foreign guest in this world. There is no mercy or leniency to be found with him; even his dog fares better than the needy soul under his roof. He considers such behavior to be right, even though it is not founded in Nature, but rather in the **Abyss** <note>original: *Abgrunde*; a term frequently used in mystical writings to describe the chaotic, dark foundation of existence where conflict and torment reside.</note>, where one form plagues, terrifies, martyrizes, and tortures another—where life acts as its own enemy.

The subordinate also learns such things from the superior, and nourishes himself with cunning and deceit, greed and malice. For if he does not employ these, he can hardly fill his belly through righteousness. So reason suggests: thus power compels him; he must increase his work and his very being and extort from his neighbor against his neighbor's sweat, without love or justice, merely so that he may fill his belly. He learns from the superior how to revel, carouse, and live the true **life of a beast** <note>original: *Vieh-leben*; a life focused solely on animal instincts and physical appetites, lacking spiritual awareness.</note>. But what the superior performs with "courtly" manners, the subordinate carries out in a beastly, swinish life and manners. Thus vice is wrought with vice, and the Devil remains Prince on Earth over both body and soul. How will you then stand when God, in His **zeal** <note>original: *Eyffer*; meaning divine jealousy or righteous wrath.</note>, shall judge the hidden things of humanity? Then every...

# A Letter to a Good Friend of Jacob Böhme’s.

->Our Salvation in the active Love<-
->**OF JESUS CHRIST**<-
->within us.<-

**M**y very dear and Christian Sir: [I wish you] the high peace, along with a heartfelt, brotherly <note>original: "gliedlicher"; literally "member-like," referring to the idea of fellow believers being members of one spiritual body.</note> desire, and a wish in active love, that the true Sun of the actual love of Jesus Christ may constantly rise and shine within your soul, spirit, and body.

**Y**our letter, dated the 24th of January, I received fourteen days after Easter, and I rejoiced in it, for I see therein that you are a hungry, zealous, and eager seeker and lover of the true Ground <term>Ground</term> <note>original: "Grundes." In Böhme’s theology, the "Ground" (or often the "Ungrund") refers to the absolute, foundational reality of God's essence and the origin of all being.</note>; which [Ground], as I understand, you have diligently pursued and investigated.

<meta>catchword: That</meta>

<summary>This page serves as the opening of a personal letter by Jacob Böhme, greeting a friend with spiritual blessings and acknowledging the receipt of a previous letter. Böhme commends the recipient's diligent search for spiritual truth and the "Ground" of being.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Epistle, Jesus Christ, soul, spirit, body, the Ground, spiritual seeker</keywords>

--- Page 262 ---
<meta>This page continues the letter from Jacob Böhme to a spiritual seeker. Böhme is responding to the recipient’s interest in his writings and explaining the necessity of moving beyond human reason to find spiritual truth.</meta>

<page-num>240</page-num>

That my writings have come into your <note>original: "ihme"; Böhme uses the formal third-person "him" to address the recipient.</note> hands and that you have found pleasure in them is certainly the result of a Divine Order, which leads those who love toward Love. It often uses strange means through which it fulfills the desire of the lover, feeding them with its gift and introducing an <term>Essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for a being or fundamental entity.</note> into their love to burn as a true fire. You should certainly believe, so long as you remain constant in the love for Truth, that she will reveal herself to you in fire-flaming love and make herself known rightly.

Only let it be started correctly: for we do not attain the true Ground of Divine Knowledge through our sharp reason and searching. Research must begin from within, in the hunger of the soul. For the searching of reason reaches only into its <term>Star</term> <note>original: "Astrum"; in Böhme's thought, this refers to the astral or governing influence of the physical world.</note> of the outer world, from which reason originates. But the soul searches in its own <term>Star</term>, as if in the inner Spiritual World, from which the visible world emerged or flowed out, and in which the soul stands with its own foundation.

But if the soul wishes to search its own <term>Star</term>, as the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; a central concept in Böhme’s work referring to the chaos or the "Great Unknown" from which God creates the universe.</note>, it must first completely surrender all its power and will to Divine Love and Grace, and first become like a child, and

<summary>Böhme explains that his writings reached the recipient through divine providence and emphasizes that true spiritual knowledge comes not from human reason, but from an internal, soulful hunger that looks beyond the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, Divine Order, Essence, Divine Knowledge, Reason, Soul, Star, Great Mystery, spiritual surrender</keywords>

through great <term>repentance</term> <note>original: "Busse"; for Böhme, this is not merely penance for sins, but a fundamental turning of the will away from the self and toward God.</note> / turn itself to its <term>Center</term> <note>original: "Centro"; a technical term in Böhme’s writing referring to the innermost point of the soul where the divine light is born.</note>

<meta>This page continues Böhme's explanation of how the Holy Spirit works within the soul. <meta>Catchword: read</meta>

It might perhaps surprise the gentleman; how a <term>layman</term> <note>original: "Leye"; refers to someone without formal university education or theological training, which Böhme famously lacked as a shoemaker.</note> could understand such high things? One who has never learned nor read them. But I say to you, my dear heart, that up until now you have seen in my writings only a glimmer of such <term>mysteries</term> <note>original: "Geheimnussen"; Böhme refers to the hidden workings of God and nature that are not accessible to the rational mind alone.</note>: for one cannot write them down: If you were to be recognized as worthy by **God**, so that the light would be ignited in your soul, then you would hear, taste, smell, feel, and see unspeakable words of **God** from such <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Erkäntnüß"; not just intellectual facts, but a lived, experiential realization of divine truth.</note>. There is the only true <term>Theosophical Pentecost-school</term> <note>original: "Theosophische Pfingstschuele"; Böhme’s term for a state of direct divine inspiration where the soul is taught by the Holy Spirit, much like the apostles at Pentecost.</note>: where the soul is taught by God.

Afterward, no searching or sharp effort is required: all gates stand open. Even a very simple person can reach it, provided he does not resist it himself with his own willing and striving: for it already lies within man, it only needs to be awakened by **God’s** Spirit.

In my <term>talent</term> <note>original: "Talento"; Böhme uses the Latin term to refer to his spiritual gift or "measure" of insight, referencing the biblical Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25:14-30).</note>, as I have been able to write it down in <term>simplicity</term> <note>original: "Einfalt"; a key virtue for Böhme, meaning a humble, child-like state of mind that is receptive to God.</note>, you will clearly see the way to this; especially in this attached booklet, which is also of my talent, and was only sent to the press a few weeks ago. Which I present to you in love, as my Christian fellow-member, and [urge] you often through to
read <note>This is the catchword "lesen" from the bottom of the original page, used to ensure the reader follows the flow to the next leaf.</note>

<summary>Böhme explains that divine wisdom is not gained through academic study but through a spiritual "Pentecost" where the Holy Spirit awakens the knowledge already latent within the human soul. He presents his own writings as a product of this "talent" or spiritual gift, offered in Christian love to the reader.</summary>

If you do not destroy this <note>The "truth" or "foundation" mentioned on the previous page.</note> with the **sectarian bickering of the scholars**, then it will become a **great tree** among you. But if you would rather bicker than **recognize the true God**: then the ray <note>A ray of divine light or spiritual inspiration.</note> passes by and hits only a few; and afterward, you will have to fetch water for the thirst of your souls from foreign peoples.

If you take this rightly into account, my writings will give you great occasion and instruction for it, and the <term>Signat-Star</term> <note>original: "Signat-Stern"; refers to the "Star of Signature." No one should look upon my person <note>Böhme frequently insists that he is merely an unlearned instrument and that readers should focus on the divine message rather than his humble status as a shoemaker.</note>; it is a pure gift of God: not for my sake alone, but also for your sake, and for all those who get to read it.

Let no one gape any longer for the **Time**; it is already born: Whom it hits, it hits; he who wakes, sees it; and he who sleeps, sees it not. It has appeared, the **Time**, and will soon appear; he who wakes sees it. Many have already felt it: But first a great <term>tribulation</term> <note>original: "Trübsahl"; a period of spiritual or worldly distress that Böhme believed preceded divine revelation.</note> must pass over, before it is entirely mani-

# Explanation of several words in the writings of J. B. <note>Jacob Böhme.</note>

## THE GREAT TURMOIL <note>original Latin: "TURBA MAGNA." In Böhme's philosophy, this is the "great disturbance" or the awakening of the "wrath-fire" within nature when the spiritual equilibrium is lost.</note>

**T**his is the stirred and awakened wrath of the inner ground, where the hellish foundation becomes manifest in the Spirit of the World: from which great plagues and illnesses arise. And it is also the awakened wrath of outward Nature, as can be seen in the terror of great thunderstorms: where the fire becomes manifest in the water. In short, it is an outpouring of God’s wrath, by which nature is <term>perturbed</term> <note>original: "turbirt." This refers to being thrown into a state of chaotic agitation or "turba."</note>.

## The Ternary <note>original Latin: "Ternarius." This refers to a grouping of three, or the manifestation of the Trinity within the created world.</note>

**I**n the word <term>Ternary</term> <note>original: "Ternarius."</note>, one understands in the <term>Language of Nature</term> <note>original: "Natur-Spraache." Böhme believed that there was a lost, primordial language where the sounds of words perfectly expressed the essence of the things they named.</note> specifically the <term>Divine Birth</term> in six forms in nature, which are the six <term>Seals of God</term>.

## The Holy Ternary <note>original Latin: "Ternarius Sanctus."</note>

**T**his is the inner heavenly working power within Being, in which the <term>Trinity of God</term> <note>original: "Dryheit Gottes," literally the "Threeness of God."</note> works: And so I understand an essential power, and the number 3 in

# THE THREE PRINCIPLES <note>original Latin: "TRIA PRINCIPIA."</note>

## 1st Principle <note>original: "1. Principium."</note>

**W**ith the 1st Principle, is indicated: The eternal Darkness / which consists in the taking-on of properties / from which sensibility <term>takes its origin</term> <note>original: "Vrständet." A specialized term in Böhme’s writing meaning to arise, to stand forth, or to find its beginning.</note> : and reaches with its foundation even into the Fire. Within this, one understands Eternal Nature and the Wrath of God.

## 2nd Principle <note>original: "2. Principium."</note>

This refers to the state of being a created, finite being or physical manifestation as opposed to the unmanifested divine essence.</note>.

## Tincture <note>original: "Tinctur." In alchemy and Böhme’s theology, a "tincture" is a spiritual, transformative power or "tingeing" essence that gives life and quality to a thing.</note>

**W**ith the word Tincture, is understood the power of Fire and Light / and the movement of this Power / [it] is called the Holy or Pure Element.

Böhme often uses this term as a play on "Qual" (torment/agony) and "Quelle" (source/wellspring), describing the restless, painful churning of the dark, spiritual foundations.</note> of such an erring spirit.

...is out of the first and second <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principio" (Latin). In Böhme's theosophy, the first principle is the dark fire-world (the root of nature), and the second is the light-world (the heart of God).</note> through the movement and out-breathing of Divine power and will; therein the spiritual world is depicted according to light and darkness, and has entered into a creaturely nature.

## Tincture.

**W**ith the word <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." In alchemy, a tincture is a substance that transforms or "colors" others. Böhme uses it to describe the vital, spiritual energy that bridges the gap between spirit and matter.</note> is understood the power of fire and light, and the movement of this power is called the Holy or Pure Element.

## C H R I S T U S .

**W**ith the word <term>Christus</term> <note>Latin: "Christ."</note> is also indicated: the <term>New Man</term> <note>original: "Newe Mensch." This refers to the spiritual rebirth of the human soul, a central theme in Böhme’s work where the "inner man" is awakened in the image of Christ.</note> in the Spirit of Christ, understood according to the inward nature.

## S A T H A N .

**W**ith the word <term>Sathan</term> <note>The Hebrew and Latin name for Satan, meaning "the Adversary."</note> is indicated: the <term>erring spirit</term> <note>original: "Irrgeist." A spirit that has wandered from the divine order or is deluded.</note>, and not always a creaturely devil, but rather the <term>tormenting source</term> <note>original: "Quall." A significant word in Böhme’s vocabulary, playing on "Qual" (torment/pain) and "Quelle" (source/wellspring), describing the agonizing, seething energy of a spirit separated from God's light.</note> of such an erring spirit.

...is from the first and second <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principio" (Latin). In Böhme's mystical philosophy, the "first principle" is the dark fire-world or the root of nature, and the "second principle" is the light-world or the heart of God.</note> through the movement and out-breathing of Divine power and will; therein the spiritual world is depicted according to light and darkness, and has entered into a creaturely nature.

## Tincture.

**W**ith the word <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." In the alchemy of Böhme's time, a tincture was a substance that could transform or "color" other things. Böhme uses it here to describe the vital, spiritual energy that bridges the gap between spirit and matter.</note> is understood the power of fire and light, and the movement of this power is called the Holy or Pure Element.

## C H R I S T .

**W**ith the word <term>Christ</term> <note>original Latin: "Christus."</note> is also indicated: the <term>New Man</term> <note>original: "Newe Mensch." This refers to the spiritual rebirth of the human soul, a central theme in Böhme’s work where the "inner man" is awakened in the image of Christ.</note> in the Spirit of Christ, understood according to the inward nature.

## S A T A N .

**W**ith the word <term>Satan</term> <note>The Hebrew and Latin name for the Adversary.</note> is indicated: the <term>erring spirit</term> <note>original: "Irrgeist." A spirit that has wandered from the divine order or is deluded.</note>, and not always a creaturely devil, but rather the <term>tormenting source</term> <note>original: "Quall." A significant word in Böhme’s vocabulary, playing on the German words "Qual" (torment or pain) and "Quelle" (source or wellspring). It describes the agonizing, seething energy that serves as the root of both life and suffering.</note>

<summary>This page defines three central terms in Böhme’s mystical system—Tincture, Christ, and Satan—explaining them as spiritual forces rather than just historical or physical entities. It positions the spiritual world as a result of the "out-breathing" of Divine power.</summary>
